# Daily Papers
The project automatically fetches the latest papers from arXiv based on keywords.

The subheadings in the README file represent the search keywords.

Only the most recent articles for each keyword are retained, up to a maximum of 100 papers.

You can click the 'Watch' button to receive daily email notifications.

Last update: 2026-11-04

## Multimodal Learning
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[VidUEU-Agent: A Data-Curation Agent for Multimodal Understanding, Editing, and Unified Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04418v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The advancement of multimodal learning is heavily constrained by the scarcity and high annotation cost of high-quality instruction data. While raw videos offer an abundant and dynamic source of visual knowledge, transforming them into precise instruction data remains highly challenging. To bridge this gap, we present VidUEU-Agent, an automated pipeline that synthesizes instruction data from raw videos for multimodal understanding, editing, and unified tasks. VidUEU-Agent follows a four-stage pipeline: it performs global perception to filter low-quality shots, mines keyframes and multimodal signals, constructs metadata and routes the metadata to synthesize instruction samples. More importantly, our agent framework can autonomously iterate the data distribution based on downstream training results, thereby optimizing data quality, balance, and training effectiveness. Scaling this pipeline, we construct VidUEU-100K, a large-scale dataset of 100K training samples spanning multiple domains and tasks, providing a robust foundation for developing comprehensive multimodal capabilities. Extensive experiments demonstrate that fine-tuning baseline models on VidUEU-100K yields significant and consistent performance gains across diverse representative benchmarks, validating the effectiveness of our proposed agent framework and dataset.</p></details> |  |
| **[Probe-Guided Gradient Balancing for Multimodal Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02651v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Jointly trained multimodal networks frequently underutilize weaker modalities, as faster-learning modalities dominate the shared fusion objective and suppress gradients reaching slower ones. Most existing approaches throttle dominant modalities, whereas directly boosting weaker modalities often fails because monitoring signals derived from the joint loss remain dominated by stronger modalities. We propose Probe-Guided Gradient Boosting (PGGB), a gradient modulation approach that boosts weaker modalities while preserving the strength of dominant ones. PGGB attaches lightweight linear classifiers to stop-gradient features from each modality to estimate representation quality independently. The gap between these scores, termed the utilization gap, provides an imbalance signal used to adaptively scale weaker-modality gradients through bounded and smoothed modulation. PGGB is composable with throttling methods, as it leaves the loss function and fusion architecture unchanged. Experiments across eight benchmarks spanning four domains and 2-4 modalities demonstrate that PGGB outperforms state-of-the-art methods on highly imbalanced multimodal datasets while remaining competitive under low or negligible imbalance. We further establish bounded scaling, self-attenuation, and a descent bound for standard SGD under smoothness and bounded-variance assumptions. Code is available at https://github.com/VuVoCodes/PGGB.</p></details> |  |
| **[Fusion or Confusion? Multimodal Complexity Is Not All You Need](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.22991v4)** | 2026-10-30 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal learning has become a prominent research area, with the potential of substantial performance gains by combining information across modalities. At the same time, model development has trended toward increasingly complex deep learning architectures, motivated by the assumption that multimodal-specific methods improve performance. We challenge this assumption through a large-scale empirical study by reimplementing 19 high-impact multimodal methods across nine diverse datasets with up to 23 modalities. Under standardized experimental conditions, including hyperparameter tuning, weight initialization, cross-validation, and statistical testing, increased multimodal complexity often yields confusion rather than effective fusion of data modalities. Accordingly, complex multimodal architectures do not reliably outperform unimodal baselines and a Simple Baseline for Multimodal Learning (SimBaMM). Through a focused case study, we further demonstrate concrete methodological shortcomings even in top-tier multimodal learning publications, underscoring the need for standardized evaluation practices. In summary, we argue for a shift in focus for multimodal learning: away from the pursuit of architectural novelty and toward methodological rigor.</p></details> |  |
| **[Soil Moisture Estimation in Precision Agriculture: A Knowledge Distillation Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40432v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurate soil moisture estimation is critical for precision agriculture, influencing irrigation, fertilization, and harvesting strategies. Traditional methods primarily rely on meteorological and soil property data, while recent approaches incorporate visual modalities such as aerial and geospatial imagery. However, these high-resolution images are expensive and computationally demanding to analyze. In this work, we propose Soil Moisture Distillation (SMD), a Knowledge Distillation (KD)-based multimodal learning framework that leverages unimodal teachers to enhance multimodal soil moisture estimation. SMD surpasses not only traditional multimodal fusion techniques, including MIS-ME, but also outperforms unimodal models, demonstrating the benefits of guided unimodal knowledge transfer. Additionally, we conduct a comparative analysis of soil patches and crop images, showing that crop images provide superior visual cues, leading to improved prediction accuracy. SMD achieves a 2% improvement in MAPE for soil patches and 1.2% for crops over state-of-the-art baselines, highlighting the effectiveness of leveraging both meteorological and visual data through knowledge distillation for enhanced soil moisture forecasting.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted by MWAIS 2025</p></details> |
| **[IMPACT: Inverter-Informed Multimodal Learning for Photovoltaic Generation Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40418v1)** | 2026-10-29 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This work develops a physics-informed multimodal deep learning framework for 10- to 110-minute-ahead probabilistic photovoltaic power forecasting. The forecasting model is built on a four-branch architecture that separately encodes plant-level temporal history and inverter-level spatial cloud dynamics through a graph attention encoder with a learned adjacency, known-future solar geometry, and the current meteorological state, fused through gated cross-attention. The geometry branch adds two forward-looking physical signals, spatial cloud heterogeneity and inverter ramp-down activity, each empirically weighted toward the horizon where it is most predictive. The decoder anchors its prediction to a smart persistence baseline, learning a horizon-conditioned correction, and the resulting quantile forecasts are calibrated online through a kernel-weighted adaptive conformal calibration step. The proposed model outperforms all 14 baselines, achieving a forecast skill score (FSS) of 0.2950, 0.099 above the next-best model. A branch-composition ablation shows that every branch contributes positively and the model retains substantial skill in challenging overcast and variable sky conditions (FSS 0.198 and 0.255).</p></details> |  |
| **[Tackling Multimodal Learning Challenges with Mixture-of-Expert: A Survey](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27431v2)** | 2026-10-28 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) presents a naturally compatible and scalable framework for multimodal learning, demonstrating strong adaptability across diverse modalities and tasks. Despite its growing success, a comprehensive and systematic review on the MoE metho addressing multimodal challenges remains lacking. Existing surveys tend to evaluate either multimodal learning or MoE independently from method taxonomy, overlooking the unique interplay between them. This survey fills that gap by answering a central question: \textit{How does MoE effectively resolve multimodal challenges?} We approach this from three key perspectives: (1) \textbf{MoE as an Efficient Multimodal Engine:} enabling scalable multimodal modeling by decoupling computational cost from parameter growth and mitigating modality redundancy through selective expert activation; (2) \textbf{MoE as a Multimodal Representation Learner:} integrating complementary multi-opinion expert knowledge to enrich alignment and interaction representations; and (3) \textbf{MoE as a Multimodal Adapter:} providing a modular and flexible mechanism to model imperfect data scenarios such as modality imbalance and missing modality. Through our extensive literature review, we identify critical research gaps, including interpretable routing, expert communication, modality integration, and lifelong multimodal learning. We position this survey as a foundation for future research toward interpretable and sustainable multimodal Mixture-of-Experts system.</p></details> | <details><summary>This ...</summary><p>This survey has been accepted by IJCAI 2026. Results were released by 30 April 2026. This latest replacement is an extension version of survey to the previous version, which has been submitted to ACM Computing Survey for peer-review</p></details> |
| **[Grounding Long Financial Text with Semantically-Enriched Tables via Multimodal Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37913v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We propose a multimodal transformer framework for efficiently modeling long1 financial documents that combine structured tabular data with noisy, unstructured2 textual disclosures. The framework represents financial tables as semantically3 enriched token sequences and integrates them with long-form text through a cross-4 attention mechanism that explicitly captures cross-modal dependencies. In par-5 ticular, we augment each numerical variable with a semantic embedding derived6 from its textual definition, enabling the model to encode both numerical magnitude7 and economic meaning. This design yields a more expressive and parameter-8 efficient representation of tabular data compared to value-only embeddings. Using9 a large-scale dataset of financial statements and MD&A disclosures, we show that10 structured numerical features remain the dominant source of predictive signal;11 however, textual information provides consistent incremental gains when incor-12 porated via cross-attention rather than simple concatenation. Our results further13 demonstrate that noisy textual disclosures are most useful when conditioned on a14 stable, semantically structured tabular representation. In particular, using frozen15 variable-level tabular representations as cross-attention queries enables the model16 to weight MD&A chunks differently for each financial variable and observation.17 These findings suggest that cross-attention provides an efficient mechanism for18 table-conditioned aggregation of long-form narrative evidence.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted to Neurips Long-context foundation model workshop</p></details> |
| **[Task-Centric Multimodal Learning for Cognitive Impairment Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20906v2)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Dementia affects millions of older adults worldwide, yet early detection remains difficult outside clinical settings. Traditional models primarily rely on text or voice-based responses, capturing only a limited view of human cognition despite the inherently multimodal nature of human expression. This work proposes a contactless, multimodal screening framework that leverages video, audio, and text signals collected during structured voice-based interactions with Amazon Alexa. By incorporating these modalities, our approach captures a more holistic representation of human behavior encompassing speech patterns, vocal characteristics, facial expressions, and linguistic structure, which may enable more accurate detection of cognitive decline. Different participants completed three conversational tasks designed to probe spontaneous speech, comprehension, and recall. We pursued three objectives: (1) evaluate which of the three Alexa tasks provides the most informative cognitive signals, (2) compare the effectiveness of unimodal models, and (3) assess the benefits of multimodal fusion models. Task-level analysis shows that the video model achieves its best performance on Command-Reading, with an accuracy of 0.65 (AUC 0.69), while the audio model performs best on Question-Answering, reaching an accuracy of 0.67 (AUC 0.73). In contrast, the multimodal model demonstrates consistently stable performance across tasks, achieving an accuracy of 0.76 (AUC 0.75) on Command-Reading and 0.76 (AUC 0.73) on Question-Answering. These findings demonstrate that multimodal behavioral cues captured through naturalistic interactions can reliably distinguish MCI from healthy aging, highlighting the limitations of traditional diagnostic paradigms and supporting the shift toward multimodal, real-world cognitive assessment frameworks.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted for publication at the 2026 IEEE Global Communications Conference (GLOBECOM)</p></details> |
| **[MultiFair: Multimodal Balanced Fairness-Aware Medical Classification with Dual-Level Gradient Modulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.07328v3)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Medical decision systems increasingly rely on data from multiple sources to ensure reliable and unbiased diagnosis. However, existing multimodal learning models fail to achieve this goal because they often overlook two critical challenges. First, various data modalities may learn unevenly, thereby converging to a model biased towards certain modalities. Second, the model may emphasize learning on certain demographic groups causing unfair performances. The two aspects can influence each other, as different data modalities may favor respective groups during optimization, leading to both imbalanced and unfair multimodal learning. This paper proposes a novel approach called MultiFair for multimodal medical classification, which addresses these challenges with a dual-level gradient modulation process. MultiFair dynamically modulates training gradients regarding the optimization direction and magnitude at both data modality and group levels. We evaluate MultiFair on three real-world medical classification datasets with diverse demographic attributes,including multiclass classification and missing-modality settings. Experimental results demonstrate its effectiveness.</p></details> | <details><summary>This ...</summary><p>This work has been accepted for publication in IEEE Transactions on Medical Imaging</p></details> |
| **[Peers4U: Designing Learner-Centered Virtual Peers for Children's Multimodal Mathematics Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37238v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In early mathematics, children learn abstract concepts through concrete representations, such as manipulating objects, drawing, and speaking. Learning with peers can enrich this process by exposing children to diverse strategies and enabling them to exchange ideas through interaction. We realized this potential with virtual peers in Peers4U, a multimodal learning system to enhance children's mathematical conceptual understanding. Virtual peers driven by LLMs are personalized to the learner's cognitive level and assigned multimodal solutions with inspectable processes. During learning, peers engage in mixed-initiative interactions by responding, initiating topics, and advancing discussions along a pedagogical progression. A user study with 22 children and two experts showed our learner-centered topic orchestration significantly outperformed a persona-centered baseline, and children spontaneously authored new artifacts to demonstrate inspired ideas or share their own strategies, producing significantly more solutions when grouped with more Leading Peers. We contribute design insights for multimodal, learner-centered virtual peer systems.</p></details> |  |
| **[ML-ECS: Heterogeneity-Aware Collaborative Multimodal Learning for Edge-Cloud Synergies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14107v2)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Edge-cloud synergies provide a promising paradigm for privacy-preserving deployment of foundation models in heterogeneous web environments, enabling lightweight edge models to support domain-specific multimodal we b services while cloud-hosted models facilitate collaborative knowledge sharing. However, in practical edge-cloud web environments, collaborative multimodal learning is challenged by modality heterogeneity (different modality combinations across domains) and model-structure heterogeneity (different modality-specific encoders/fusion modules). To address these issues, we propose ML-ECS, a collaborative multimodal learning framework that enables joint training between a server-based model and heterogeneous edge models. This framework consists of four components: (1) cross-modal contrastive learning (CCL) to align modality representations in a shared latent space, (2) adaptive multimodal tuning (AMT) to preserve domain-specific knowledge from local datasets, (3) modality-aware aggregation (MAA) to robustly aggregate while mitigating noise caused by missing modalities, and (4) SLM-enhanced CCL (SE-CCL) to facilitate bidirectional knowledge transfer between cloud and edge. Experimental results on various multimodal tasks show that ML-ECS consistently outperform state-of-the-art baselines under varying modality availability, achieving improvements of 1.55% to 16.75% in Rouge-LSum and improving both client- and server-side performance. In addition, by communicating only low-rank LoRA parameters and fused representations, ML-ECS achieves high communication efficiency, requiring only 0.71% of the total parameter volume.</p></details> |  |
| **[VaViT: Vanilla ViT for Automotive Point Cloud Semantic Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31177v2)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Plain Transformers have become the de-facto architecture for processing text, audio, image, and video, offering a unified backbone for multimodal learning. However, state-of-the-art architectures for point cloud semantic segmentation remain dominated by U-Nets architectures where convolutions are interleaved with local or windowed attentions. In this work, we show how to effectively leverage vanilla, non-hierarchical ViTs for the segmentation of large-scale automotive lidar scenes. We bridge the performance gap thanks to a carefully designed tokenizer, a lightweight decoder segmentation head, and tailored data augmentations. Our approach, VaViT, for Vanilla ViT, matches or exceeds the performance of state-of-the-art methods while maintaining the simplicity of ViT architecture. We provide extensive evaluations on nuScenes, SemanticKITTI, and Waymo Open Dataset to validate the efficiency of our method. Code and pre-trained models are available at https://github.com/valeoai/VaViT.</p></details> | WACV 2027 |
| **[Ampe-DB: A Dataset for Human Activity and Interaction Analysis Using the Ghanaian Game Ampe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33130v1)** | 2026-10-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Human activity recognition benchmarks underrepresent culturally situated forms of movement and interaction. We introduce Ampe-DB, a multimodal dataset based on Ampe, a traditional Ghanaian game involving coordinated jumping, leg movements, and two-person interaction. It contains 637 canonical clips from 15 participants, with RGB video, two-person pose representations, participant and pair metadata, four-class movement labels, and derived binary movement-relation labels. We evaluate four-class movement recognition and binary movement-relation classification using three protocols: a duplicate-safe fixed benchmark, four-fold pair-held-out evaluation, and participant-held-out diagnostics for participants with complete class coverage. Baselines comprise a frozen Kinetics-400-pretrained R(2+1)D-18 RGB encoder, a two-layer bidirectional GRU for pose sequences, and validation-selected RGB-pose late fusion. For four-class recognition, RGB macro-F1 falls from 0.955 on the fixed benchmark to 0.418 under pair-held-out evaluation and 0.384 under participant-held-out evaluation. Pose is more robust, achieving 0.957, 0.811, and 0.839 across the same protocols. Fusion reaches 0.976 on the fixed benchmark and 0.824 under pair-held-out evaluation, but does not consistently improve on pose under participant shift. Binary movement-relation recognition also becomes substantially harder under held-out evaluation, especially for different-leg configurations. These findings show that strong fixed-benchmark scores can mask failures to generalize across people and pairings. Ampe-DB supports research on fine-grained movement recognition, human-human interaction, multimodal learning, and identity-aware generalization.</p></details> | <details><summary>74 pa...</summary><p>74 pages, 9 figures, 7 numbered tables. Ampe-DB V1 dataset (637 interaction clips) available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/RuthBiney/Ampe-DB</p></details> |
| **[Reliability-Aware Probabilistic Fusion for Multimodal Learning with Incomplete Evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29621v1)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal inputs may be missing or degraded, raising distinct questions of evidence availability and trust. We introduce a reliability-aware Gaussian Product-of-Experts (PoE) in which an input-dependent reliability weight tempers each modality's latent precision, while missing experts are omitted. We characterize how reliability and availability affect posterior information and expert influence. Across two clinical multimodal tasks, the full framework remains competitive with complete inputs and achieves its clearest mean-AUROC advantage under modality absence, particularly on HAIM-MIMIC-MM. Ablations show a substantial gain from missing-aware training and an additional HAIM improvement from reliability weighting under matched PoE, missing-aware, and consistency training. Unseen test-time corruptions are used diagnostically and reveal modality-dependent reliability-weight and latent-uncertainty responses, including failure under severe degradation. Thus, reliability weighting provides an explicit evidence-control mechanism, while learning dependable quality signals remains an open challenge.</p></details> |  |
| **[On the Complementarity of Quantum and Classical Features: Adaptive Hybrid Quantum-Classical Feature Fusion for Breast Cancer Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.22903v2)** | 2026-10-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The integration of quantum machine learning with classical deep learning offers promising avenues for medical image analysis by mapping data into high-dimensional Hilbert spaces. However, unifying these paradigms remains challenging due to optimization asymmetries. We propose a hybrid quantum-classical architecture for breast cancer diagnosis based on a dual-branch feature-extraction pipeline. Our framework unifies complementary representations from classical models and trainable or deterministic (non-trainable) quantum circuits. Three fusion strategies are introduced: Static Hybrid Fusion (SHF) for offline extraction, Dynamic Hybrid Fusion (DHF) for end-to-end co-adaptation, and Temperature-Scaled Hybrid Fusion (TSHF). Inspired by multimodal learning, TSHF uses a learnable scalar to balance branch gradient dynamics and address optimization bottlenecks. We evaluate BreastMNIST, INbreast, and BUS-UCLM, spanning ultrasound and mammography, using ResNet-18 and a shallow CNN, with five independent runs per configuration. Fusion improves over the strongest classical baseline on all three benchmarks. On BreastMNIST, TSHF with ResNet-18 and a non-trainable circuit achieves 88.33% accuracy, 92.09% F1-score, and 92.09% AUC-ROC, versus 84.74% accuracy and 87.08% AUC-ROC for ResNet-18 alone. On INbreast, SHF with the same pairing performs best, at 70.42% accuracy, 72.96% F1-score, and 80.28% AUC-ROC. On BUS-UCLM, SHF with a trainable circuit achieves 78.46% accuracy and 84.66% F1-score, over seven accuracy points above the best standalone branch. Adaptive fusion is not universally beneficial: static fusion performs best on the two smaller datasets, suggesting an additional estimation burden from co-adaptation and learned calibration under limited data. Clinical applicability requires validation beyond the small, low-resolution datasets and simulated quantum circuits considered here.</p></details> | <details><summary>41 pa...</summary><p>41 pages, 16 figures. This manuscript is a preprint under review at Artificial Intelligence in Medicine</p></details> |
| **[Beyond Invariance: Factor-wise Selective Treatment of Shifts for Multimodal Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21403v2)** | 2026-10-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Distribution shifts pose a fundamental challenge to robust multimodal learning. Existing methods typically promote invariance by suppressing environment-dependent variations, implicitly applying a uniform treatment to shifted information. However, shifted information can have different predictive consequences: some contributes little to prediction, some remains reliably informative, and some exhibits environment-dependent predictive effects. This raises a fundamental question: which shifted information should be suppressed, preserved, or attenuated? To address this question, we propose FaShift, a factor-wise shift framework that diagnoses latent representation factors according to their distributional changes, intervention effects, and cross-environment stability. By factorizing multimodal representations and evaluating these complementary properties, FaShift distinguishes non-predictive factors, stably predictive factors, and predictively useful but unstable factors. It then selectively suppresses, preserves, or attenuates their contributions, rather than enforcing uniform invariance. Experiments on MOSI, MOSEI, and CH-SIMS show that FaShift achieves the best ID accuracy and F1 across all three datasets. Compared with the strongest competitors reporting OOD results, it improves OOD accuracy/F1 by 1.5/3.6 points on MOSI and 1.7/2.7 points on MOSEI, while achieving the best reported OOD accuracy and F1 on CH-SIMS.</p></details> |  |
| **[Reusing Multimodal Agents: Efficient Evaluation of Sound Designs in Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27670v1)** | 2026-10-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper introduces a novel framework for efficiently evaluating in-game sound designs using multimodal learning and reinforcement learning. Sound design significantly influences gameplay and accessibility; however, objective evaluation methods, defined here as automated assessments based on agent performance rather than human ratings, often require retraining agents from scratch for each sound design. Our approach first pre-trains a multimodal encoder that encodes visual and auditory information into a shared latent space. A reinforcement learning agent is then trained using this latent representation. We fine-tune the multimodal encoder for each sound design while keeping the trained policy fixed, allowing us to isolate the effects of sound changes on agent behavior. To evaluate the proposed method, we conducted experiments on games from multiple genres, comparing the performance of blind agents (agents using only sound input) under various sound designs. Experimental results suggest that this approach can detect differences in agent performance caused by degraded or altered sound design. Moreover, we significantly reduce the computational cost compared to retraining the agent from scratch. Experimental results suggest that this approach provides a practical and scalable solution for evaluating in-game sound designs. The source code, data and supplementary document are available: https://github.com/thainv0212/tog-reusing-agents.</p></details> |  |
| **[VLX-Go: Robust Embodied Tracking through Multi-Stage Learning and On-Policy Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19361v2)** | 2026-10-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Embodied visual tracking requires a robot to identify a designated target from visual observations and control its own motion to follow that target through an environment. Following an isolated person is a comparatively simple demonstration of embodied visual tracking, but following the intended person among distractors remains challenging. Existing methods exhibit a substantial drop in success from single-target to distractor tracking, motivating a focus on the shared spaces that person-following robots must navigate. We present VLX-GO, a vision-language waypoint policy built around the hypothesis that visual and language representations should be aligned before tracking-specific motion is learned. The policy combines complementary frozen visual encoders, multiscale temporal context, a transformer backbone, and a lightweight head for robot-centric waypoint prediction. Its training progresses through vision--language alignment, multimodal reasoning mid-training, and tracking specialization on 19,532 oracle episodes. Compared with 41.54% distractor-tracking success for our earlier OmTrackVLA model, the complete multi-stage recipe achieves 55.80%. We then explore on-policy refinement using filtered oracle labels for states visited by the policy. This preliminary study adds 2.42 percentage points of distractor-tracking success, reaching 58.22%, while reducing collision rate by 1.99 pts. The final policy records the highest distractor- and ambiguous-tracking success rates among the compared methods. These results support staged multimodal learning as a promising direction for tracking among distractors and motivate further study of on-policy refinement for person following in shared spaces. Code is available at https://github.com/om-ai-lab/VLX-Go.</p></details> | tech report |
| **[CheXFuse: Guided Differential Joint Attention Fusion for Robust Multimodal Chest X-Ray Classification in Data-Scarce Medical Settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25617v1)** | 2026-10-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>A central challenge in multimodal learning is how to fuse modalities that differ substantially in predictive strength. When one modality provides a much stronger representation than another, indiscriminate cross-modal interaction can introduce weak, redundant, or unstable signals, limiting or even degrading performance relative to the strongest unimodal representation. To address this problem, we propose CheXFuse, a multimodal fusion framework based on Guided Differential Joint Attention (GuDJA). CheXFuse combines image and text token representations within a joint multimodal sequence, where learned token guidance emphasizes informative representations and differential attention reduces less useful attention responses. We evaluate CheXFuse on the Indiana University (IU) Chest X-ray dataset, using binary (normal vs. abnormal) classification as the downstream task. Experiments are conducted under both original-report and masked-report settings, with the latter serving as a controlled stress test under reduced textual informativeness. CheXFuse with GuDJA achieves the strongest mean overall performance across the evaluated baselines and ablations, reaching 95.73% accuracy, 95.79% F1-score, 96.54% recall, and 95.28% sensitivity at 95% specificity (Sens@95Spec) with original reports, and 95.58%, 95.60%, 95.60%, and 94.34%, respectively, under masked reports. These results suggest that GuDJA provides a promising fusion strategy under asymmetric or partially reduced modality informativeness.</p></details> | IEEE ICDM DMBIH 2026 |
| **[MM-LeJEPA: Correlated Gaussian Regularization for Multimodal Joint-Embedding Predictive Architectures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24681v1)** | 2026-10-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>LeJEPA derives an isotropic Gaussian embedding target by minimizing, under its task assumptions, a Fisher information criterion that controls the task-averaged squared prediction bias. This enables unimodal self-supervised learning without reconstruction decoders, exponential-moving-average teachers, or contrastive negatives. However, its isotropic task prior does not account for the cross-modal structure of multimodal tasks that read shared content. We introduce MM-LeJEPA by extending LeJEPA's task assumptions to multimodal learning. Specifically, we model multimodal tasks as strength-weighted combinations of components that read independent information from the two modalities and components that read the same content from both. Under explicit gradient-moment, correspondence, and symmetry assumptions, we derive a correlated Gaussian target that minimizes a weighted Fisher information functional under a fixed total variance constraint. An analytic transform adapts Sketched Isotropic Gaussian Regularization (SIGReg) to this target, yielding a two-term objective that combines distributional regularization with local-to-global prediction. The extension adds a single scalar hyperparameter, the target cross-modal correlation, and retains the simplicity of LeJEPA. On public audio-visual classification benchmarks, our results are competitive with representative open-source methods. Code will be released.</p></details> |  |
| **[AdamPM: Memory-Efficient LLM Optimization via Moment Pooling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24598v1)** | 2026-10-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Adam is widely used for training large language models (LLMs) owing to its strong optimization performance and training stability, but its element-wise estimation for the first and second moments incur substantial memory overhead. Although numerous memory-efficient optimizers have been proposed, many incur additional computational overhead or require additional tuning of hyperparameters. Consequently, Adam remains a dominant and reliable choice for LLM pre-training. We propose AdamPM (Adam with Pooled Moments), a simple and memory-efficient variant of Adam that groups adjacent moment entries along the row or column dimension and represents each group by a single shared mean. AdamPM can well inherit Adam's hyperparameter settings and is readily integrated into existing Adam optimizer implementations, with less memory overhead. We systematically investigate pooling of both first and second moment estimates and identify second moment pooling as the effective design. With a group size of $s$, AdamPM reduces second moment memory to $1/s$ of the original requirement. Extensive experiments on language model pretraining, fine-tuning and multimodal learning demonstrate that AdamPM maintains model performance and training speed while substantially reducing optimizer-state memory. Our code is available at \url{https://github.com/jixiaowei95/AdamPM}.</p></details> | 19 pages |
| **[MSCR: Jointly Balancing Utilization Across Modalities and Discovering Synergistic Information](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24395v1)** | 2026-10-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In multimodal learning, modality imbalance often lets one modality dominate optimization and keeps the model from fully exploiting cross-modal interactions. Balance-oriented methods such as the Multimodal Competition Regularizer (MCR) perform well by alleviating this imbalance during training. These dynamic balancing methods, however, mainly adjust modality contributions and do not consider how imbalance affects the formation of synergistic information during fusion. Synergy is a major source of multimodal advantage, because it is predictive gain that cannot be recovered from unimodal evidence alone. We propose the Multimodal Synergistic Competition Regularizer (MSCR), a fusion method that jointly promotes balanced modality utilization and the discovery of synergistic information. MSCR has two complementary objectives. An anti-dominance objective suppresses excessive unimodal influence and leaves room for synergistic information, and a synergy objective encourages the fused predictor to outperform a non-synergistic unimodal reference. An adaptive gate further concentrates the regularization on samples that need stronger collaborative modeling. Experiments on five benchmarks show that MSCR consistently improves performance over MCR and strengthens synergistic capability. It raises conditional recovery, the accuracy of the fused prediction on samples that the unimodal predictors misclassify, as well as the share of the model's output that InterSHAP attributes to cross-modal interactions. Its gain in conditional recovery persists when the fusion module is Transformer-based. Code is available at https://github.com/chenxinyu991-collab/MSCR.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted at NeurIPS 2026</p></details> |

| **[FedSSMCoOp: SSM Encoders for light-weight Federated Prompt Learning for Few-shot Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09907v1)** | 2026-10-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have shown strong performance across a wide range of downstream vision tasks, thanks to the complementary information contained in the respective domains. To overcome this issue, we propose FedSSMCoOp, a federated few-shot image classification framework that enables multimodal learning while preserving data privacy. | **[Improving Fake Profile Detection Through Multimodal Learning and Research-Guided Feature Integration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23926v1)** | 2026-10-06 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Fake accounts continue to undermine online platforms, yet it remains unclear whether multimodal learning offers meaningful improvements over strong metadata baselines or graph-based approaches. To address these issues, we propose an Alignment- and Calibration-driven Multimodal Learning framework (ACML). Specifically, ACML incorporates a dynamic cross-modal triplet alignment module, which enforces strong semantic consistency for high-confidence positive pairs while encouraging diverse representation learning between high- and low-confidence positive pairs according to their confidence gaps. Additionally, ACML introduces a difference-aware attention calibration strategy that adaptively adjusts attention regularization based on feature magnitude and confidence differences across modalities, thereby mitigating biases caused by unreasonable fusion constraints. | **[Constant-Curvature Sliced Gromov-Wasserstein for Heterogeneous Cross-Curvature Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07218v1)** | 2026-10-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Recent advances in representation learning have highlighted the utility of constant-curvature models, such as hyperbolic and spherical spaces, for modeling complex data. Consequently, they lack explicit mechanisms to enforce geometric consistency across various spaces. Moreover, the problem of comparing probability distributions across mixed-curvature spaces remains unexplored. To compare distributions on heterogeneous spaces, Gromov-Wasserstein (GW) distances provide a principled framework by aligning their intra-space geometries. Building on this, we propose constant-curvature sliced Gromov-Wasserstein (CCSGW), a novel divergence for aligning distributions supported on heterogeneous constant-curvature spaces. We provide theoretical analysis showing that CCSGW controls intrinsic geometric discrepancy across heterogeneous spaces, promoting distribution-level geometric consistency. By integrating CCSGW into existing mixed-curvature learning tasks, including graph anomaly detection, graph node classification, and multimodal learning, we observe consistent performance gains across diverse settings.</p></details> |  |

| **[From Models to Systems: A Comprehensive Survey of Efficient Multimodal Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19445v1)** | 2026-09-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The rapid expansion of multimodal models has surfaced formidable bottlenecks in computation, memory, and deployment, catalyzing the rise of Efficient Multimodal Learning (EML) as a pivotal research frontier. Furthermore, we provide a holistic discussion and application-specific optimization blueprints for diverse domains and posit a paradigm shift toward self-regulating intelligence, where efficiency is an intrinsic, emergent property of the model's fundamental design rather than a post-hoc constraint. Finally, we present open challenges and future directions that will define the trajectory of EML research. This survey establishes a structured framework for multimodal systems that are not only high-performing and generalizable but natively efficient and ready for ubiquitous deployment. We further discuss how emerging multimodal foundation models instantiate these principles at scale and identify open challenges including scalable information estimation in high-dimensional settings, standardized evaluation across information-theoretic methods, combinatorial complexity of multimodal PID, and the transition from post-hoc information analysis toward information-aware multimodal learning.</p></details> |  |

These same properties also delimit what cosine can express. Normalization discards radial variation; anisotropic representations can compress angular contrast; high-dimensional neighborhoods can develop hubs; and a symmetric, uncalibrated score may be mismatched to the relation of interest. This article offers a selective review organized around a simple principle: the usefulness of cosine similarity depends jointly on the learned representation, any normalization or post-processing, the scoring rule, and the downstream decision. | **[LAION-BVD: A 10-Million-Hour Open Video Dataset for Multimodal Pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24845v1)** | 2026-08-25 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present LAION-BVD, a large-scale open video dataset for multimodal learning, which contains 1.3B platform-specific video URLs collected from CommonCrawl. The CMA output then passes to a causal temporal model for forecasting. Evaluated across ten random seeds, DSA achieves a median test MSE of 0.416, versus 0.668, 0.607, and 0.851 for iTransformer, TaTS, and GPT4MTS, corresponding to mean-error reductions of 54.95%, 37.29%, and 67.23%, with paired Cohen's d of 0.555, 0.337, and 0.345, respectively, and ranks first in 100% of bootstrap draws. | **[Mitigating Sample-Level Imbalance via Probabilistic Separation for Adaptive Multimodal Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.21797v4)** | 2026-08-24 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal learning faces modality imbalance, where dominant modalities suppress weaker ones due to inconsistent convergence rates. In the Adaptive Training stage, a GMM-guided Adaptive Loss dynamically reallocates optimization priorities, imposing stronger modality alignment penalties on imbalanced samples while prioritizing multimodal fusion for balanced ones. Experimental results demonstrate that our method significantly outperforms current state-of-the-art baselines. | **[Towards Automated Discovery: A Review of Generative Models, Multimodal Learning and Closed-Loop Workflows in Inverse Materials Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02507v2)** | 2026-08-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Inverse materials design is shifting materials discovery from forward prediction toward targeted proposal of candidates that satisfy objectives under physical constraints. We also discuss multimodal learning combining crystal structures, thermodynamic and electronic information, microscopy, spectroscopy, processing context, and scientific text to construct materials representations. To support credible claims, we define a nine-rung discovery-credibility ladder and propose a minimum reporting standard: declared matching tolerances and database snapshots; separate reporting of uniqueness, training-set memorization, and external rediscovery; novelty as a continuous distance distribution; energy-above-hull distributions with functional and hull version; relaxation-survival and dynamical stability rates; and validation cost per credible hit. | **[Prototype-based Self-Supervised Multimodal Learning for PPG and Accelerometry Signals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09764v3)** | 2026-08-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Modeling multi-modal time-series data is critical for capturing system-level dynamics, particularly in biosignals where modalities such as ECG, PPG, EDA, and accelerometry provide complementary perspectives on interconnected physiological processes. ## Representation Learning
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |

| **[Diagnosis by Composition: Detecting and Localizing Unseen Diseases by Composing Learned Radiological Findings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05231v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Deep models for radiological diagnosis are typically trained to predict disease labels directly from images, which limits them to diseases seen during training and makes their decisions hard to interpret in radiologists' terms. Radiology, however, proceeds from visual findings to disease through published guidelines that make this decision rule explicit, interpretable, and reproducible. We introduce diagnosis by composition (DbC), a representation learning framework that encodes this findings-first logic. DbC learns a vocabulary of radiological findings by aligning image features with the text embeddings of the findings, each finding represented as a dense map over the scan. A disease is defined by a set of required findings derived from its guidelines, and its diagnosis map is a fixed, parameter-free composition of the corresponding finding maps. Because the finding-to-disease mapping is derived from guidelines rather than learned, DbC is interpretable and auditable by design, can extend to new diseases by specifying their findings without retraining, and can diagnose unseen diseases by composing their findings. On multi-phase liver CT, DbC detects and localizes unseen diseases, rare diseases from a different institution, and an external cohort significantly better than all comparators, including models that align images with disease names. DbC also improves detection and localization in the fully supervised setting and remains superior across training-set sizes. Although demonstrated on liver CT, DbC applies wherever radiological guidelines define diseases through findings.</p></details> |  |
| **[Community detection in complex networks via node similarity, graph representation learning, and hierarchical clustering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.12212v3)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Community detection is a significant challenge in analysing real graphs, including social, citation, biological, and many other networks. This article proposes three new, general, hierarchical frameworks to deal with this task. The introduced approach supports various linkage-based clustering algorithms, vertex proximity matrices, and graph representation learning models. We compare 364 module combinations on Stochastic Block Model graphs and real-life datasets. We observe that our best pipelines (Wasserman-Faust and the mutual information-based PPMI proximity, as well as the deep learning-based DNGR representations) perform competitively with the state-of-the-art Leiden and Louvain algorithms. At the same time, unlike the latter, they remain hierarchical. Thus, they output a series of nested partitions of all possible cardinalities which are compatible with each other. This feature is crucial when the number of correct partitions is unknown in advance, and we show that it is obtained at near-zero practical cost in terms of partition quality. On graphs composed of many small communities, our pipelines are more accurate than the reference solutions. Nevertheless, the downside of our approach is its time complexity, which confines the framework to networks of some 10000 vertices on personal machines.</p></details> |  |
| **[Basin2Vec: Learning Basin-Centric Embeddings for Scalable Hydrological Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05164v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reliable streamflow prediction across time and in ungauged basins requires representations that capture hydrologically relevant basin characteristics. Existing methods rely on either fixed handcrafted attributes or general-purpose Earth observation embeddings not tailored to basin structure. We introduce \textbf{Basin2Vec}, a self-supervised framework that learns compact basin representations from hydroclimatic and physiographic data without observed streamflow. Basin2Vec organizes representation learning around basin scale and relationships across space and time. Across multiple forecasting architectures, it consistently outperforms predefined attributes and general-purpose geospatial embeddings in temporal generalization and zero-shot spatial transfer. Basin2Vec provides average relative gains of up to 8.3% temporally and achieves 3.5x the median predictive performance of the strongest baselines on unseen basins. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of basin-centric pretraining for transferable hydrologic prediction. Code is available at https://github.com/Muhammad-Talha-MT/basin2vec</p></details> |  |
| **[Dataset Recommendation for Heterogeneous and Dynamic Scholarly Graphs via Multimodal Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05071v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Researchers struggle to find relevant datasets amid the growing volume of data across repositories and scholarly publications. Manual dataset retrieval is time-consuming and often fails to identify valuable resources that are not easily discoverable through direct links or popularity metrics. Dataset recommendation, which aims to identify datasets relevant to a paper, author, or query, has become essential for improving dataset discoverability and reuse. However, this task remains challenging because Scholarly Knowledge Graphs (SKGs) are large-scale, heterogeneous, dynamic, and incomplete. Although openly available SKGs organize scientific knowledge by linking scholarly outcomes, they often lack meaningful connections between datasets, leaving them isolated and underutilized. These graphs contain millions of evolving relations but still suffer from missing or inaccurate metadata, duplicate records, and weak dataset integration. This work presents the Multimodal Scholarly Attention Network (MM-SAN), a heterogeneous multimodal graph representation learning method for recommending datasets relevant to research publications within real-world SKGs. MM-SAN combines topology-aware and text-based embeddings, enabling effective recommendations even when textual metadata is limited or absent. It operates in transductive, semi-inductive, and fully inductive settings, adapting to evolving SKGs without requiring retraining. Experiments show that MM-SAN outperforms state-of-the-art methods, providing a robust and scalable solution for dataset recommendation.</p></details> |  |
| **[CausalBind: Causal Modeling and Learning for Protein-Molecule Virtual Screening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07340v2)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Protein-molecule virtual screening is increasingly cast as a problem of representation learning in a shared embedding space. Existing methods rely on dense holistic alignment, entangling invariant binding determinants with nuisance correlations and limiting transfer to new targets. Binding in protein-molecule systems involves sparse cross-modality interactions: binding is governed by a small contact interface and a few decisive local interactions (e.g., hydrogen bonds, hydrophobic contacts, and salt bridges) rather than the global structures of the protein and molecule. We hypothesize that uncovering and leveraging sparse interaction patterns is critical for generalization beyond the training data, as these patterns are reusable and expected to improve performance across different scenarios. Since the training data contain only observed binding pairs, we formalize this prior via a V-structure causal model under Heckman-style selection, and establish three theoretical results: (i) when the compatibility between concepts is bilinear, the latent concepts of interacting proteins and molecules are not identifiable, even if the interaction is sparse; (ii) with a non-bilinear compatibility function, these concepts and their sparse interaction support are component-wise identifiable under a sparse antichain condition; and (iii) a low-rank relaxation yields subspace identifiability of the interaction modes, and applies to bilinear compatibility as well. Inspired by these principles, we propose CausalBind with three implementation variants. Extensive experiments on DUD-E and LIT-PCBA benchmarks show that all variants consistently outperform strong retrieval baselines, with the largest gains on LIT-PCBA early enrichment, and further generalize to target- and scaffold-level out-of-distribution splits. Code and scripts are freely available at https://github.com/lokali/CausalBind.</p></details> | NeurIPS 2026 (Oral) |
| **[UniFlow: Unified Multi-Scale Flow Matching for Time Series Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04825v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Real-world time series exhibit complex dynamics across multiple temporal resolutions, yet effectively incorporating multi-scale information into generative modeling remains challenging without increasing generative complexity. We propose UniFlow, a unified framework that integrates multi-scale representation learning with efficient non-autoregressive generation through a shared-semantic discrete interface. Specifically, a multi-scale tokenizer employs shared-index voting to consolidate complementary temporal information into a single public-ID sequence, while scale-specific codebooks preserve the information required for reconstruction. Building on this unified representation, we develop geometry-aware categorical flow matching, which initializes transport from learned low-rank anchors and generates public identities non-autoregressively. This design captures multi-scale temporal structure without requiring multiple generative token streams or sequential token decoding. Experiments across four benchmarks and both short- and long-term settings demonstrate improved generative fidelity, strong cross-scale consistency, and substantial inference speedups over representative autoregressive and diffusion-based baselines. | **[TAPES: Task-Aligned Joint Predictive Pretraining for Transaction Event Sequences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04747v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Transaction event sequences support diverse prediction tasks in online commerce and digital financial services. Moreover, target-wise prediction losses do not explicitly model conditional co-occurrence among targets. To address these challenges, we propose TAPES, a task-aligned joint predictive pretraining framework for transaction sequences. TAPES constructs a finite collection of candidate target sets from admissible field-operator combinations. It selects a target set according to the downstream utility of representations learned through short joint pretraining trials under matched budgets. It then reinitializes the encoder and pretrains it on the selected targets through a joint likelihood parameterized by a shared discrete latent variable, before downstream supervised adaptation. Experiments in a real-world transaction-risk setting and on multiple public financial benchmarks spanning diverse downstream tasks demonstrate that TAPES outperforms representative baselines. Existing approaches often encode visual and tactile observations independently before fusion, limiting their ability to capture fine-grained cross-modal dependencies. Moreover, most methods focus on observations at the current time step and overlook the temporal evolution of contact. VT-MUSE addresses both limitations through a two-stage representation learning framework. In Stage I, modality specific encoders are jointly adapted via cross-modal temporal alignment and masked-view consistency. In Stage II, a conditional variational latent model processes masked visual sequences together with full tactile histories. Auxiliary decoders reconstruct the masked recent visual observations and predict tactile depth changes, encouraging the latent representation to retain both global visual context and local contact dynamics. On the simulation benchmark, VT-MUSE outperforms the strongest baseline evaluated on all tasks by 11 percentage points and also achieves substantial improvements in real-world experiments.</p></details> |  |
| **[MambaHand: Local Identity and Behavioral Sequence Learning with Mamba for Dynamic Hand Gesture Authentication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04626v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Dynamic hand gesture authentication (DHGA) is an emerging biometric technique that contains both physiological and behavioral identity characteristics. | **[E2I-Flow: EEG-to-Image Reconstruction via Wavelet-Temporal Cross-Attention and Conditional Flow Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04524v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Electroencephalography (EEG)-based visual decoding and reconstruction aims to recover visual information from non-invasive brain signals. | **[The Implicit Bias of Hyperbolic Representation Learning for Multiclass Data: A Busemann Risk Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07131v2)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We study the implicit bias of Riemannian gradient flow for hyperbolic multiclass classification with fixed class prototypes in hyperbolic space $\mathbb H^n$. | **[Pairwise Distortion Distribution with Graph-Based Importance Sampling for Quantization Error and Intrinsic Dimension Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04341v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Quantifying the dimensionality of data is a fundamental need in machine learning and signal processing applications such as representation learning, data compression, and inverse problems, where the extrinsic dimension in which the data is presented can be very different from the intrinsic dimension that determines the operational performance. These results demonstrate that high-fidelity defect synthesis can meaningfully accelerate NPI by enabling effective inspection models before sufficient real defect data has been collected.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted to the Future Technologies Conference (FTC) 2026. To appear in Springer Lecture Notes in Networks and Systems (LNNS). 17 pages, 6 figures, 4 tables</p></details> |
| **[Geometric Morphology Learning: Empowering Foundation Models from Structure to Spacetime Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01932v1)** | 2026-11-01 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Geometry is the very form that connects space, time, and matter, serving as the fundamental fabric of the physical world, from continuous fields to discrete structures. Despite its importance, deep learning is inherently blind to geometry. Riemannian geometry stands as a well-established mathematical foundation for studying geometry, yet it remains largely incompatible with deep learning architectures. To bridge this gap, we establish a new learning framework--Geometric Morphology Learning--that learns geometric or topological structures, shapes or conformations for representation learning, reasoning and action modeling. Moving beyond early attempts limited to specific geometric priors, this theory centers on modeling the intrinsic geometry of objects and learning from such geometric or topological knowledge. Specifically, we construct a neural bundle, a unified scaffolding linking local and global structures, and formulate differentiable operators, including connection and curvature, thereby enabling the study of geometry with advanced geometric concepts such as holonomy and cohomology. | **[From Prediction to Action: Population Health World Model for Digital Medicine](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24662v2)** | 2026-10-30 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Digital medicine now captures data that traces individual health trajectories and reveals population health dynamics through electronic health records, claims, remote monitoring, wearable devices, mobile devices, social sensing data, medical images, etc. But much of its modeling still centers on task-specific, outcome-specific, and prediction-specific estimation. The next frontier is not simply better prediction, but action in an evolving health world shaped by multiple decision-makers, heterogeneous populations, resource constraints, and feedback in care delivery. ## Causal Inference
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[Causal Inference from Self-controlled Case Series](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03531v2)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The self-controlled case series (SCCS) design is widely used in studies of rare events, for example of vaccine side effects. Conceptually, the design has two main features. First, it requires only data from individuals who have experienced the event of interest. Therefore, the design is especially useful when the outcome is rare and the available data come from surveillance or clinical records, say, of hospitalized patients. Second, because comparisons are made within single individuals, time-invariant confounding between treatment and outcome is eliminated. We formulate a set of sufficient identification assumptions for estimating the causal effect of vaccination on an adverse outcome under the SCCS design, drawing on ideas from the causal inference literature. Unlike the existing SCCS literature, we do not consider a parametric model as the starting point. This allows us to establish when and why the SCCS design allows identification of causal effects, using data from cases only. However, because the sufficient identifiability assumptions are implausible in some applications, we also develop statistical tests for the existence of a treatment effect that remain valid when only a subset of the identification assumptions holds.</p></details> |  |
| **[Debiased Estimating Equation Method for Robust and Efficient Mendelian Randomization Using a Large Number of Correlated Weak and Invalid Instruments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.05386v3)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Mendelian randomization (MR) is a widely used tool for causal inference in the presence of unmeasured confounders, which uses single nucleotide polymorphisms (SNPs) as instrumental variables to estimate causal effects. However, SNPs often have weak effects on complex traits, leading to bias in existing MR analyses when weak instruments are included. In addition, existing MR methods often restrict analysis to independent SNPs via linkage disequilibrium clumping and result in a loss of efficiency in estimating the causal effect due to discarding correlated SNPs. To address these issues, we propose the Debiased Estimating Equation Method (DEEM), a summary statistics-based MR approach that can incorporate a large number of correlated, weak-effect, and invalid SNPs. DEEM effectively eliminates the weak instrument bias and improves the statistical efficiency of the causal effect estimation by leveraging information from a large number of correlated SNPs. DEEM also allows for pleiotropic effects, adjusts for the winner's curse, and applies to both two-sample and one-sample MR analyses. Asymptotic analyses of the DEEM estimator demonstrate its attractive theoretical properties. Through extensive simulations and two real data examples, we demonstrate that DEEM significantly improves the efficiency and robustness of MR analysis compared with existing methods.</p></details> |  |
| **[A Financial Brain Scan of the LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21285v3)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We use sparse autoencoders (SAEs) to "brain scan" large language models, mapping internal activations to interpretable concepts and enabling targeted "steering" of specific features. We show that prompting models to adopt specific preferences activates many unintended concepts, complicating causal inference. In contrast, SAEs permit smooth, more isolated steering of target concepts. Furthermore, our scans reveal a stark disconnect between the model's internal feature contributions and its self-generated explanations, showing that these explanations are unreliable guides to its reasoning. Finally, SAEs provide a lightweight intervention for existing models, producing interpretable embeddings and allowing researchers to attenuate specific economic biases.</p></details> | 82 pages |
| **[A Practical Causal Framework for Autonomous Mobile Robots: From Simulation to Real-World Human Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04027v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Causal inference has emerged as a powerful paradigm enabling robots to make safe, efficient decisions by reasoning about the ``why'' behind events. To this end, we tackle the challenge of transferring simulation-learnt causal relations to the real world with minimal effort. While retaining the invariant high-level causal structure, we empirically demonstrate the system adapts efficiently to real-world situations by updating only the causal model parameters with a limited set of new observations. Our results show that the parameter update enables rapid sim-to-real transfer. The framework runs efficiently onboard of a mobile robot and significantly outperforms a non-causal baselines navigating successfully and safely in complex human-shared scenarios.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted in IEEE Robotics and Automation Letters (RA-L)</p></details> |

Across image-based benchmarks, IMMD-Flow matches or improves upon state-of-the-art generative approaches while showing significant speed advantages.</p></details> |  |

| **[Inference for Batched Adaptive Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.10156v2)** | 2026-11-01 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The advantages of adaptive experiments have led to their rapid adoption in economics, other fields, and among practitioners. | **[Whose Guardrail? | **[trajectory-judge: What Outcome-Only LLM Judges Miss on Agent Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00038v4)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>A direct test of an LLM judge of agent trajectories injects faults into correct runs and reports recall, per fault type or by whether the fault broke the environment outcome (loud) or not (silent). Our testbed, a deterministic support desk with a scripted oracle and a one-step fault injector, labels all 400 trajectories exactly. A 14B judge shown only the request and final reply scores 34% to 76% recall on four fault types that leave the reply unchanged. There its input is the clean run's, so its paired discrimination is zero and that recall is its flag rate on clean runs. Splitting by outcome survival does not fix this: its loud recall of 84% is a paired +0.393 and its silent recall of 45% a paired +0.048, all from the two fault types that change the reply. Told to check each step, the same model flags every fault of those four types and 0 of 100 clean runs (95% CI up to 3.6%). It does not reliably check the reply: of four invented promises it flags one every time and the other three once in 42 faults. Shown every step but asked only about the reply, it still reaches a paired +0.69 on reply-unchanged faults, against +1.00 when told to check each step. We recommend reporting paired discrimination against clean parents, split by whether the fault reaches the judge's input and by outcome survival, and release the testbed, raw verdicts and analysis pipeline.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted at the NeurIPS 2026 Workshop: Who Verifies the Agents? Contrary to theoretical expectations, changing only the numerical scale of payoffs substantially alters cooperation. Moreover, payoff scale modifies the effects of persona instructions: at sub-unit stakes, where no payoff shown exceeds 1, all five models cooperate more under a selfish than a cooperative persona, whereas Claude and Gemini reverse this pattern at larger scales. Predictive renormalization acts on the complete conditional training law for a single pass over distinct corpus target blocks, retaining optimizer memory, remaining data, schedule, and numerical policy. Autonomous reductions require closure; approximate reductions carry successor and emission errors. Such attacks are difficult to detect because no new action is introduced, while parameter changes themselves are often legitimate parts of the user's request. Our key insight is that the legitimacy of a parameter change should be determined by the trust of the information flow that produces it under the user query. The query induces a task-specific trust hierarchy over available information fields: some are trusted to determine particular parameters, while others should have little or no influence. Based on this insight, we propose AuthorityFlow, a three-stage parameter-level defense. First, it interprets the user query to construct a task-specific trust hierarchy over the information fields available to the agent. Second, for each tool argument, it traces the information flow that produces its current value and attributes the value to its supporting field. Finally, it accepts the value only when that field is trusted to determine the parameter under the current task, allowing high-trust changes while flagging low-trust influence as potential parameter poisoning. We further introduce FactAuthBench for evaluating parameter poisoning in LLM agents. Without defense, the attack success rate reaches 93.06%. Author affiliation: School of Chemistry and Life Resources, Renmin University of China. AI-driven change was the second-most frequent concern about the field's future 20.2%, yet only 7.0% of responses mentioned AI when researchers were asked to identify one change the community should make. ROCm InferaSim benchmarks each distinct set of kernels once, on at most one node. We call this measurement an anchor. Analytical laws carry that anchor to other deployments that run the same kernels. For multi-node deployments, ROCm InferaSim reduces the configuration to a single-node equivalent, measures it, and scales the result analytically. To reduce communication cost, Eco-MobiLLM develops an activation shortcut that transmits only the tokens involved in the loss calculation instead of full activation matrices to guide the side network tuning. Besides, to improve computing efficiency and further accelerate FT convergence, Eco-MobiLLM introduces a novel two-stage training paradigm, which allows the server to reuse historical activations and saves the mobile device from repeatedly computing forward passes for the recurring data samples. | **[Extracting Search Trees from LLM Reasoning Traces Reveals Myopic Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06840v6)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Large language models (LLMs), especially reasoning models, generate extended chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning that often contains explicit deliberation over possible futures. By fitting computational models to the extracted search trees, we quantify how plans are structured and how they influence move decisions. We find that LLMs search less deeply than humans, and that their performance is better predicted by search breadth than depth. Most strikingly, although LLMs expand deep nodes in their traces, their move choices are best explained by a myopic model that ignores those nodes entirely. A causal intervention that selectively prunes CoT paragraphs supports that move selection depends predominantly on shallow rather than deep nodes. These patterns contrast with human planning, where performance is driven primarily by deep search. Together, our findings reveal a key difference between LLM and human planning: human expertise rests on deeper search, whereas LLMs generate deep lookahead but fail to act on it. This dissociation offers concrete targets for aligning LLM and human planning. More broadly, our framework provides a generalizable approach for interpreting the structure of LLM planning across strategic domains.</p></details> |  |

| **[VeriHarm-Bench: Measuring Harmful Success in LLM Coding Agents Verifiably](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03575v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Large language model (LLM) coding agents increasingly operate with shell access, allowing them to write scripts, move files, alter permissions, and modify system configurations. | **[Can LLM Agents Be Forward-Deployed Engineers? | **[RogueMerge: Robust and Unified Attacks against LLM Model Merging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03344v3)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Model merging composes specialized capabilities into a single LLM by aggregating task-specific LLMs sourced from unverified public platforms, exposing a critical supply-chain attack surface: Because any malicious behavior can be encoded into a task vector, and merging grants third-party vectors direct write access to model weights, an attacker-provided task vector can enable or amplify diverse downstream threats. (ii) Attackers have no knowledge of the victim's merging configurations, causing a static attack vector optimized in isolation to be easily diluted or neutralized. (iii) Practical threat induction must generalize to attack prompts unseen during optimization, which static vectors cannot adequately encode. In light of these gaps, we present RogueMerge, the first unified attack framework addressing all three challenges. To handle autoregressive generation, we replace static arithmetic with a joint optimization that explicitly enforces attack success after merging. To better simulate unknown merging settings, we formulate attack injection as a stochastic min-max optimization problem and solve it via meta-gradient descent. To generalize across heterogeneous attack prompts, we employ distributionally robust optimization and derive a tractable first-order Taylor approximation at LLM scale, with a provable error bound. Across four threats, six merging algorithms, and over 170 merged LLMs, RogueMerge consistently outperforms prior attacks. It also remains stable across diverse merging settings and resists standard defenses.</p></details> | <details><summary>To ap...</summary><p>To appear in IEEE Symposium on Security and Privacy (S&P), 2027</p></details> |

| **[IAMRAGBENCH: Benchmarking Authorization Expansions in LLM-Generated AWS IAM Policies under RAG Poisoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03431v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Large language models (LLMs) using retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) can synthesize Amazon Web Services (AWS) Identity and Access Management (IAM) policies from tasks and operational documents. | **[IdeaTaste: Do LLMs Share the Research Taste of Human Researchers?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03424v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Research taste, the judgment of what to read, which problems to pursue, and which methods to try, increasingly shapes the work of AI scientists, yet it remains far less understood than their ability to execute research. LLM agents fall well short of human communities in both the literature they cite and the ideas they state, reaching about 26\% of the human level on citation matching and only 18\% on idea matching. Their populations can cite as diversely as a community and take up its tasks and methods, yet rarely arrive at the concrete approaches and research plans it pursues. Neither richer field context nor larger populations substantially close this gap. In blinded expert review, AI proposals trail the 2026 papers least in presentation and most in expected success, echoing their gap in concrete approaches, and those whose ideas no human paper shares fare worst, suggesting weaker directions rather than overlooked opportunities. These results indicate that the main bottleneck in AI research taste lies less in finding the directions a community works on than in turning them into the concrete approaches it goes on to pursue. Moreover, we observe significant cross-lingual differences in bias behaviour for all except the most accurate models. With the release of MBBQ, we hope to encourage further research on bias in multilingual settings. The dataset and code are available at https://github.com/Veranep/MBBQ.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted to COLM 2024</p></details> |
| **[R$^2$-Omni: Reducing Structural and Precision Redundancy in Omni-LLMs for Long-Form Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03247v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Omni-modal large language models (Omni-LLMs) typically process large numbers of multimodal tokens from audio-visual inputs, incurring substantial computational costs and a large memory footprint for the key-value (KV) cache.
